<p>
<strong>The lambda calculus</strong> is so simple that it doesn't seem to be computing at all, yet it's as powerful as any CPU.
          </p>

<p>
<strong>The halting problem</strong> is easy to state: "will a given function terminate or not?" No computer can solve it.
          </p>
</div>

<p>
<strong>The Chomsky hierarchy</strong> relates programming languages, state machines, regexes, and linguistics.
          </p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="row">
<div class="twelve columns">
<p>
          All computing systems follow these rules and many others, <strong>both in theory and in practice</strong>.
          Ten dense screencasts cover the major computational topics <strong>using code, but no mathematical notation</strong>.
          </p>

  // Choose the best video size for this screen resolution.
  $(function() {
    var height = window.screen.availHeight * window.devicePixelRatio;
    var width = window.screen.availWidth * window.devicePixelRatio;
    /* iPad Minis have a resolution of 2048x1536, but the iPad Mini 3 can't
       play 4k video. Test against 2048 rather than 1920 here so that we don't
       feed 4k to those devices. */
    var resolution = Math.max(height, width) <= 2048 ? "1080p" : "4k";

    var source = document.createElement('source');
    if (resolution == "4k") {
      source.src = "/screencasts/catalog/introduction-to-computation/download?resolution=4k";
    } else if (resolution == "1080p") {
      source.src = "/screencasts/catalog/introduction-to-computation/download?resolution=1080p";
    }
    $("#video-24822bd98838fa28").append(source);
  });

  // Focus the video element when it's clicked or played
  // (The play event is handled explicitly because clicking on the play control
  // doesn't actually generate a click event for the video tag.)
  $(function() {
    $("video, video *").on("click play", function() {
      $(this).closest('video').focus();
    });
  });

      The 90 classic Destroy All Software screencasts explain advanced programming practices via concrete examples: <strong>testing</strong>, <strong>design</strong>, fluency with tools like <strong>Unix shells</strong>, and other topics.

</h1>
<p>
<a href="https://www.executeprogram.com">Execute Program</a> is a learning platform that teaches you programming tools quickly, using spaced repetition to ensure that you remember them.
        Courses are made up primarily of code examples, not text, and all code examples are interactive.
        A typical course contains hundreds of small examples, slowly increasing in complexity.
      </p>
<p>
        Full access to <a href="https://www.executeprogram.com">Execute Program</a> is included with all Destroy All Software subscriptions.
      </p>

<div class="row">
<div class="twelve columns">
<p>
      Destroy All Software subscriptions cost <strong>$29 per month</strong>, which grants access to both the back catalog and new releases.
      </p>
<p>
      The <strong>format</strong> may change over time: screencasts, text, or maybe something entirely new.
      This is the outlet for Gary's thoughts about software development in whatever form they take.
      Currently, new <a href="/screencasts/catalog">screencasts</a> are being published.
      </p>
<p>
      If the schedule or the format change in ways that you don't like, don't worry: Destroy All Software has a permissive <strong>refund policy</strong>.
      You can always cancel from the website; we won't make you send an awkward email to support.
      You can also <a href="mailto:support@destroyallsoftware.com">email support</a> for a refund within a month of your most recent charge.
      </p>
